Формула \(Q\), local finiteness/minimum spacing of active prime nodes, centered Toeplitz/Rayleigh bridge, A1′ density, A2 continuity, and the old compact-to-global closure are all already present in Q3【81:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L14-L18】【81:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L37-L49】【81:3†RH_Q3.pdf†L46-L100】【81:7†RH_Q3.pdf†L25-L50】【81:9†RH_Q3.pdf†L10-L29】【81:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L55-L76】【81:1†RH_Q3.pdf†L22-L34】.

1. Notation

Fix \(K>0\).  
For \(F\in C_c(\mathbb R)\), define the symmetrically extended Weil functional
\[
\mathcal Q(F)
:=
\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)F(\xi)\,d\xi
-
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\bigl(F(\xi_n)+F(-\xi_n)\bigr),
\qquad
\xi_n:=\frac{\log n}{2\pi}.
\]
For even \(F\), one has \(\mathcal Q(F)=Q(F)\).

For \(\Phi\in C_c(\mathbb R)\), write
\[
\widetilde{\Phi}(x):=\overline{\Phi(-x)}.
\]

2. Lemma P1 (exact packet sesquilinear identity)

Let \(\Psi,\Phi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2])\). Define
\[
K_Q(\Psi,\Phi):=\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde{\Phi}).
\]
Then
\[
K_Q(\Psi,\Phi)
=
\iint_{\mathbb R^2} a^*(x-y)\Psi(x)\overline{\Phi(y)}\,dx\,dy
-
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(
\langle \Psi,T_{\xi_n}\Phi\rangle
+
\langle \Psi,T_{-\xi_n}\Phi\rangle
\Bigr),
\]
where \(T_\xi\Phi(x):=\Phi(x-\xi)\).

Proof.  
Since
\[
(\Psi*\widetilde{\Phi})(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb R}\Psi(x)\overline{\Phi(x-\xi)}\,dx,
\]
substitute this into \(\mathcal Q\), interchange integrals by Fubini, and use that \(\Psi*\widetilde{\Phi}\) is supported in \([-K,K]\), so only finitely many prime nodes contribute.

3. Proposition P2 (Toeplitz reduction on a translation packet dictionary)

Let \(g\in C_c(\mathbb R)\), \(\Delta>0\), and
\[
g_j(x):=g(x-j\Delta),\qquad
\Psi_c:=\sum_{j=-M}^{M} c_j g_j.
\]
Set
\[
h:=g*\widetilde g.
\]
Then
\[
\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c}
=
\sum_{i,j=-M}^{M} c_i\overline{c_j}\,h(\cdot-(i-j)\Delta).
\]
Hence
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
=
\sum_{i,j=-M}^{M} c_i\overline{c_j}\,\kappa_{i-j},
\qquad
\kappa_m:=\mathcal Q\bigl(h(\cdot-m\Delta)\bigr).
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
=
\langle T_M(\kappa)c,c\rangle_{\mathbb C^{2M+1}},
\qquad
T_M(\kappa):=[\kappa_{i-j}]_{|i|,|j|\le M}.
\]

Proof.  
Expand \(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c}\) and use
\[
g_i*\widetilde{g_j}=h(\cdot-(i-j)\Delta),
\]
then apply linearity of \(\mathcal Q\).

4. Proposition P3 (desired prime-block factorization; sufficient but not available)

Define the prime kernel
\[
K_P(\Psi,\Phi)
:=
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(
\langle \Psi,T_{\xi_n}\Phi\rangle
+
\langle \Psi,T_{-\xi_n}\Phi\rangle
\Bigr).
\]
If there exist a Hilbert space \(\mathcal H_{P,K}\) and a linear map
\[
J_K:\mathcal P_K\to\mathcal H_{P,K}
\]
on a dense packet space \(\mathcal P_K\subset C_c([-K/2,K/2])\) such that
\[
K_P(\Psi,\Phi)=\langle J_K\Psi,J_K\Phi\rangle_{\mathcal H_{P,K}}
\qquad
\forall \Psi,\Phi\in\mathcal P_K,
\]
then \(K_P\) is positive semidefinite on \(\mathcal P_K\).

Proof.  
Immediate.

5. Proposition P4 (obstruction: the prime block is not PSD on dense translation packet spaces)

Assume \([-K,K]\) contains at least one active prime node \(\xi_{n_0}>0\). Then the prime kernel \(K_P\) is not positive semidefinite on any translation packet space rich enough to contain arbitrarily narrow translates of a nonzero packet.

Proof.  
Choose \(g\not\equiv0\) with support so small that
\[
\operatorname{supp}g,\quad
\operatorname{supp}g(\cdot-\xi_{n_0}),\quad
\operatorname{supp}g(\cdot-2\xi_{n_0})
\]
are pairwise disjoint and, for every active \(\xi_n\neq \xi_{n_0}\), all overlaps relevant to \((\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})(\xi_n)\) vanish. This is possible because the active nodes in \([-K,K]\) are finite and have positive minimum spacing.

Set
\[
\Psi:=g-g(\cdot-\xi_{n_0}).
\]
Since \(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi}\) is even,
\[
K_P(\Psi,\Psi)
=
\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\,(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})(\xi_n).
\]
For \(n\neq n_0\), the chosen support separation gives
\[
(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})(\xi_n)=0.
\]
At \(n=n_0\),
\[
(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})(\xi_{n_0})
=
\langle \Psi,T_{\xi_{n_0}}\Psi\rangle
=
-\|g\|_2^2<0.
\]
Therefore
\[
K_P(\Psi,\Psi)
=
-\frac{2\Lambda(n_0)}{\sqrt{n_0}}\|g\|_2^2<0.
\]
So \(K_P\) is not PSD.

6. Lemma P5 (Archimedean–prime decomposition of the Toeplitz coefficients)

With the notation of Proposition P2, define
\[
\alpha_m:=\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)\,h(\xi-m\Delta)\,d\xi,
\]
and
\[
\beta_m
:=
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(
h(\xi_n-m\Delta)+h(\xi_n+m\Delta)
\Bigr).
\]
Then
\[
\kappa_m=\alpha_m-\beta_m.
\]

Proof.  
Apply the definition of \(\mathcal Q\) to \(h(\cdot-m\Delta)\), noting that \(h\) is even.

7. Proposition P6 (Toeplitz–Herglotz criterion)

Fix \(g,\Delta\), and let \((\kappa_m)_{m\in\mathbb Z}\) be as above. The following are equivalent:

(i) For every \(M\ge0\),
\[
T_M(\kappa)=[\kappa_{i-j}]_{|i|,|j|\le M}\succeq0.
\]

(ii) The sequence \((\kappa_m)\) is positive-definite on \(\mathbb Z\), i.e.
\[
\sum_{i,j=1}^N c_i\overline{c_j}\,\kappa_{m_i-m_j}\ge0
\]
for all finite choices of indices \(m_i\in\mathbb Z\) and coefficients \(c_i\in\mathbb C\).

(iii) There exists a finite positive measure \(\mu_{g,\Delta}\) on \(\mathbb T\) such that
\[
\kappa_m=\int_{\mathbb T} e^{-im\theta}\,d\mu_{g,\Delta}(\theta)
\qquad
(m\in\mathbb Z).
\]

If, in addition, \((\kappa_m)\in \ell^1(\mathbb Z)\), then these are also equivalent to

(iv) The Fourier series
\[
S_{g,\Delta}(\theta):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}\kappa_m e^{-im\theta}
\]
converges uniformly and satisfies
\[
S_{g,\Delta}(\theta)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \theta\in\mathbb T.
\]

Proof.  
Standard Toeplitz–Herglotz theorem on \(\mathbb Z\).

8. Proposition P7 (full-symbol domination criterion)

Assume \(g,\Delta\) are such that the sequences \((\alpha_m)\) and \((\beta_m)\) admit Fourier representations as finite measures on \(\mathbb T\):
\[
\alpha_m=\int_{\mathbb T} e^{-im\theta}\,d\mu_A(\theta),
\qquad
\beta_m=\int_{\mathbb T} e^{-im\theta}\,d\mu_P(\theta).
\]
If
\[
\mu_A-\mu_P \ge 0
\]
as a finite positive measure on \(\mathbb T\), then \((\kappa_m)\) is positive-definite, hence
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})\ge0
\qquad
\forall c\in\mathbb C^{2M+1},\ \forall M.
\]

In the stronger \(\ell^1\)-regime, if
\[
A_{g,\Delta}(\theta):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}\alpha_m e^{-im\theta},
\qquad
P_{g,\Delta}(\theta):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}\beta_m e^{-im\theta}
\]
converge uniformly and satisfy
\[
A_{g,\Delta}(\theta)\ge P_{g,\Delta}(\theta)
\qquad
\forall \theta\in\mathbb T,
\]
then the same conclusion holds.

Proof.  
Since
\[
\kappa_m=\alpha_m-\beta_m=\int_{\mathbb T} e^{-im\theta}\,d(\mu_A-\mu_P)(\theta),
\]
the positivity of \(\mu_A-\mu_P\) implies that \((\kappa_m)\) is positive-definite by Proposition P6. The \(\ell^1\)-version is the same statement with densities \(A_{g,\Delta}\) and \(P_{g,\Delta}\).

9. Theorem P8 (conditional corrected compact positivity)

Fix \(K>0\). Let \(\mathcal P_K\subset C_c([-K/2,K/2])\) be a dense pre-packet space such that
\[
\overline{
\operatorname{cone}\{\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi}:\Psi\in\mathcal P_K\}
}^{\|\cdot\|_\infty}
=
\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}.
\]
Assume, moreover, that \(Q\) is continuous on compact windows and that Proposition P7 holds for every admissible translation dictionary contained in \(\mathcal P_K\). Then
\[
Q(\Phi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Phi\in\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}.
\]

Proof.  
By Proposition P7,
\[
Q(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})=\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Psi\in\mathcal P_K.
\]
Hence \(Q\ge0\) on the dense packet-autocorrelation cone. By continuity of \(Q\) on compact windows, positivity extends to the closure, i.e. to \(\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}\).

10. One-line verdict

The strict Route P chain is:

P1 exact sesquilinear identity  
\(\to\) P2 Toeplitz reduction  
\(\to\) P3 desired prime PSD factorization  
\(\to\) P4 obstruction: prime PSD is false  
\(\to\) P5 full split \(\kappa=\alpha-\beta\)  
\(\to\) P6 Toeplitz–Herglotz criterion  
\(\to\) P7 full-symbol domination  
\(\to\) P8 corrected compact positivity.

So the real frontier theorem is not “prime block is PSD”, but:
\[
\boxed{
\mu_A-\mu_P\ge0
\quad\text{or, in symbol form,}\quad
A_{g,\Delta}(\theta)\ge P_{g,\Delta}(\theta)\ \forall\theta,
}
\]
for every admissible packet dictionary.

If you want, the next move is to write the exact LaTeX-ready regularized version of P7, with the minimal hypotheses on \(g\), \(\Delta\), and the regularization needed to make \(A_{g,\Delta}\) a genuine symbol rather than only a distribution.

1. Setup

Fix \(K>0\).  
Let
\[
\mathcal Q(F)
:=
\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)F(\xi)\,d\xi
-
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\bigl(F(\xi_n)+F(-\xi_n)\bigr),
\qquad
\xi_n=\frac{\log n}{2\pi}.
\]
For even \(F\), one has \(\mathcal Q(F)=Q(F)\) in the notation of Section 7【83:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L18-L35】【83:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L78-L108】.

Let \(g\in C_c(\mathbb R)\), and for \(\Delta>0\) define
\[
g_j(x):=g(x-j\Delta).
\]
Let \(J\subset\mathbb Z\) be a finite index set such that
\[
\operatorname{supp}(g_j)\subset[-K/2,K/2]
\qquad
\forall j\in J.
\]
Set
\[
\widetilde g(x):=\overline{g(-x)},
\qquad
h:=g*\widetilde g.
\]
For \(c=(c_j)_{j\in J}\in\mathbb C^J\), define
\[
\Psi_c:=\sum_{j\in J} c_j g_j.
\]
Let
\[
D(J):=\{i-j:\ i,j\in J\}.
\]

2. Lemma P7.1 (exact finite Toeplitz reduction)

For every \(c\in\mathbb C^J\),
\[
\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c}
=
\sum_{i,j\in J} c_i\overline{c_j}\,h(\cdot-(i-j)\Delta).
\]
Hence
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
=
\sum_{i,j\in J} c_i\overline{c_j}\,\kappa_{i-j},
\qquad
\kappa_m:=\mathcal Q\bigl(h(\cdot-m\Delta)\bigr),
\quad m\in D(J).
\]

Proof.  
Use
\[
g_i*\widetilde{g_j}=h(\cdot-(i-j)\Delta)
\]
and linearity of \(\mathcal Q\).

3. Lemma P7.2 (Archimedean–prime coefficient split)

For \(m\in D(J)\), define
\[
\alpha_m:=\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)\,h(\xi-m\Delta)\,d\xi,
\]
and
\[
\beta_m
:=
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(
h(\xi_n-m\Delta)+h(\xi_n+m\Delta)
\Bigr).
\]
Then
\[
\kappa_m=\alpha_m-\beta_m.
\]

Proof.  
Apply the definition of \(\mathcal Q\) to \(h(\cdot-m\Delta)\), noting that \(h\) is even.

Remark.  
This is well-defined because \(h(\cdot-m\Delta)\) is compactly supported in \([-K,K]\) for \(m\in D(J)\), so only finitely many prime nodes contribute【83:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L37-L49】.

4. Proposition P7.3 (finite symbol identity)

Define the finite trigonometric polynomials
\[
A_J(\theta):=\sum_{m\in D(J)} \alpha_m e^{-im\theta},
\qquad
P_J(\theta):=\sum_{m\in D(J)} \beta_m e^{-im\theta},
\]
and
\[
S_J(\theta):=A_J(\theta)-P_J(\theta)
=\sum_{m\in D(J)} \kappa_m e^{-im\theta}.
\]
For
\[
p_c(\theta):=\sum_{j\in J} c_j e^{ij\theta},
\]
one has
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi} S_J(\theta)\,|p_c(\theta)|^2\,d\theta.
\]

Proof.  
By Lemma P7.1,
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
=
\sum_{m\in D(J)} \kappa_m
\sum_{\substack{i,j\in J\\ i-j=m}} c_i\overline{c_j}.
\]
On the other hand,
\[
|p_c(\theta)|^2
=
\sum_{i,j\in J} c_i\overline{c_j}e^{i(i-j)\theta},
\]
so Fourier orthogonality on \(\mathbb T\) gives the claim.

5. Corollary P7.4 (exact finite-symbol domination criterion)

If
\[
S_J(\theta)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \theta\in[-\pi,\pi],
\]
equivalently
\[
A_J(\theta)\ge P_J(\theta)
\qquad
\forall \theta\in[-\pi,\pi],
\]
then
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})\ge0
\qquad
\forall c\in\mathbb C^J.
\]
Hence the Toeplitz block
\[
T_J(\kappa):=[\kappa_{i-j}]_{i,j\in J}
\]
is positive semidefinite.

Proof.  
Immediate from Proposition P7.3.

This is the **exact** P7 you want on a fixed finite dictionary.  
No distribution theory is needed.

6. Proposition P7.5 (Poisson-regularized verification criterion)

For \(0<r<1\), let \(P_r\) be the Poisson kernel on \(\mathbb T\). Define
\[
A_{J,r}:=P_r*A_J
=
\sum_{m\in D(J)} r^{|m|}\alpha_m e^{-im\theta},
\]
\[
P_{J,r}:=P_r*P_J
=
\sum_{m\in D(J)} r^{|m|}\beta_m e^{-im\theta},
\]
and
\[
S_{J,r}:=A_{J,r}-P_{J,r}=P_r*S_J.
\]
Then
\[
\|S_J-S_{J,r}\|_\infty
\le
(1-r)\sum_{m\in D(J)} |m|\,|\kappa_m|
\le
(1-r)\sum_{m\in D(J)} |m|\bigl(|\alpha_m|+\beta_m\bigr).
\]

Proof.  
Since
\[
S_J(\theta)-S_{J,r}(\theta)
=
\sum_{m\in D(J)}(1-r^{|m|})\kappa_m e^{-im\theta},
\]
use
\[
1-r^{|m|}\le (1-r)|m|
\]
and take sup norms.

7. Corollary P7.6 (regularized domination with explicit error budget)

Assume there exist \(0<r<1\) and \(\varepsilon_J>0\) such that
\[
A_{J,r}(\theta)-P_{J,r}(\theta)\ge \varepsilon_J
\qquad
\forall \theta\in[-\pi,\pi],
\]
and
\[
(1-r)\sum_{m\in D(J)} |m|\bigl(|\alpha_m|+\beta_m\bigr)\le \varepsilon_J.
\]
Then
\[
S_J(\theta)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \theta\in[-\pi,\pi],
\]
hence
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})\ge0
\qquad
\forall c\in\mathbb C^J.
\]

Proof.  
By Proposition P7.5,
\[
S_J(\theta)\ge S_{J,r}(\theta)-\|S_J-S_{J,r}\|_\infty\ge \varepsilon_J-\varepsilon_J=0.
\]
Apply Corollary P7.4.

8. Theorem P7.7 (conditional corrected compact positivity)

\[
\overline{
\operatorname{cone}\{\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi}:\Psi\in\mathcal P_K\}
}^{\|\cdot\|_\infty}

\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}.
\]

\[

\]

Proof.

\[
Q(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})=\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde{\Psi})\ge0

\forall \Psi\in\mathcal P_K.

## 1. Параметры, которые реально нужны

Берём один base packet \(g\in C_c(\mathbb R)\), пусть
\[
\operatorname{supp} g \subset [-R_g,R_g],\qquad
h:=g*\widetilde g,\qquad \widetilde g(x):=\overline{g(-x)}.
\]
Тогда
\[
\operatorname{supp} h \subset [-R_h,R_h],\qquad R_h:=2R_g,
\]
и если \(g\ge0\), то \(h\ge0\), \(\|h\|_1=\|g\|_1^2\), \(\|h\|_\infty\le \|g\|_2^2\).

Для finite translation dictionary \(J\subset\mathbb Z\) и шага \(\Delta>0\) положим
\[
g_j(x):=g(x-j\Delta),\qquad
\Psi_c:=\sum_{j\in J} c_j g_j.
\]
Тогда, как уже установлено,
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
=
\sum_{i,j\in J} c_i\overline{c_j}\,\kappa_{i-j},
\qquad
\kappa_m=\alpha_m-\beta_m,
\]
где
\[
\alpha_m:=\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)\,h(\xi-m\Delta)\,d\xi,
\]
\[
\beta_m:=\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(h(\xi_n-m\Delta)+h(\xi_n+m\Delta)\Bigr).
\]

## 2. Archimedean bounds for \(\alpha_m\)

### Lemma A1 (support-localization bound)
Пусть
\[
I_m:= [m\Delta-R_h,\ m\Delta+R_h].
\]
Тогда
\[
|\alpha_m|
\le
\|a^*\|_{L^\infty(I_m)}\,\|h\|_1.
\]

Доказательство:
\[
\alpha_m=\int_{-R_h}^{R_h} a^*(m\Delta+u)\,h(u)\,du,
\]
потому что \(h\) supported in \([-R_h,R_h]\). Берём sup on \(I_m\).

Это базовая off-diagonal envelope.

---

### Lemma A2 (approximate-identity refinement)
Если \(I_m\subset[-K,K]\), то
\[
\bigl|\alpha_m-a^*(m\Delta)\|h\|_1\bigr|
\le
\omega_{a^*,K}(R_h)\,\|h\|_1,
\]
где
\[
\omega_{a^*,K}(\rho):=
\sup_{\substack{x,y\in[-K,K]\\ |x-y|\le \rho}}
|a^*(x)-a^*(y)|.
\]

Следовательно
\[
|\alpha_m|
\le
\bigl(|a^*(m\Delta)|+\omega_{a^*,K}(R_h)\bigr)\|h\|_1.
\]

Это уже **практический** bound: если \(h\) narrow, то \(\alpha_m\) почти sample’ит \(a^*(m\Delta)\).

---

### Lemma A3 (packet core/tail lower bound for the diagonal)
Фиксируем \(r\in(0,R_h]\). Положим
\[
H_r:=\int_{|u|\le r} h(u)\,du,
\qquad
T_r:=\|h\|_1-H_r,
\]
\[
m_r^*:=\inf_{|x|\le r} a^*(x),
\qquad
M_{r,K}^*:=\sup_{r<|x|\le R_h}|a^*(x)|.
\]
Тогда
\[
\alpha_0
\ge
m_r^*\,H_r - M_{r,K}^*\,T_r.
\tag{A-diag}
\]

Это — точный packet analogue of old core/off-core lower bound from Section 8【86:2†RH_Q3.pdf†L34-L45】【86:11†RH_Q3.pdf†L1-L25】.

Если \(h\) сильно сосредоточен возле нуля, то \(H_r\) близко к \(\|h\|_1\), и diagonal Arch contribution large and positive.

---

### Lemma A4 (crude positive-core version)
Если \(R_h\le r\), то
\[
\alpha_0\ge m_r^*\,\|h\|_1.
\]
Это самый сильный локальный вариант: весь \(h\) сидит внутри зоны, где \(a^*\) uniformly positive.

## 3. Prime bounds for \(\beta_m\)

Пусть
\[
\Xi_K:=\{\xi_n:\ 0<\xi_n\le K\}
\]
— set of active positive nodes on \([0,K]\). By Lemma 7.1 this set is finite and has minimum spacing
\[
\delta_K:=\min_{\xi_m\ne \xi_n\in \Xi_K} |\xi_m-\xi_n|>0.
\]
【86:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L37-L49】

Введём local symmetric prime mass:
\[
\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(s;R)
:=
\frac12\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(
\mathbf 1_{|\xi_n-s|\le R}
+
\mathbf 1_{|\xi_n+s|\le R}
\Bigr),
\qquad s\ge0.
\]

### Lemma P1 (local prime-mass bound)
Для любого \(m\in\mathbb Z\),
\[
0\le \beta_m \le \|h\|_\infty\,\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(|m|\Delta;R_h).
\tag{P-loc}
\]

Доказательство: в каждой summand
\[
h(\xi_n\mp m\Delta)\le \|h\|_\infty
\]
и она ненулевая только если \(|\xi_n\mp m\Delta|\le R_h\).

---

### Lemma P2 (spacing simplification)
Если
\[
R_h<\frac{\delta_K}{2},
\]
то каждая band \([s-R_h,s+R_h]\) contains at most one active node. Поэтому
\[
\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(s;R_h)\le w_{\max}(K),
\qquad
w_{\max}(K):=\max_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}.
\]
Следовательно
\[
\beta_m\le \|h\|_\infty\,w_{\max}(K).
\tag{P-sp}
\]

Это уже грубо, но часто удобно.

---

### Lemma P3 (vanishing criterion)
Если \(|m|\Delta>R_h\) и
\[
\operatorname{dist}(|m|\Delta,\Xi_K)>R_h,
\]
то
\[
\beta_m=0.
\tag{P-van}
\]

То есть off-diagonal prime coefficients **исчезают**, если dictionary-spacing deliberately avoids all active prime nodes by more than the support radius of \(h\).

Это главный выигрыш от local finiteness + spacing【86:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L37-L49】.

## 4. Finite-symbol lower bounds

Пусть \(D(J)=\{i-j:\ i,j\in J\}\), \(D_+(J):=D(J)\cap\mathbb N\).  
Так как \(h\) even и \(a^*\) even, имеем
\[
\alpha_{-m}=\alpha_m,\qquad \beta_{-m}=\beta_m.
\]

Для finite symbol
\[
S_J(\theta)=\sum_{m\in D(J)}(\alpha_m-\beta_m)e^{-im\theta}
\]
получаем pointwise envelope:
\[
S_J(\theta)
\ge
\alpha_0-\beta_0
-
2\sum_{m\in D_+(J)}\bigl(|\alpha_m|+\beta_m\bigr).
\tag{S-env}
\]

Значит достаточно доказать
\[
\alpha_0-\beta_0
>
2\sum_{m\in D_+(J)}\bigl(|\alpha_m|+\beta_m\bigr).
\tag{C0}
\]
Тогда \(S_J(\theta)\ge0\) for all \(\theta\), и по finite-symbol criterion
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})\ge0
\qquad \forall c\in\mathbb C^J.
\]

Это важнее, чем старая Poisson-regularized form: на конечном dictionary можно **вообще не regularize**.

## 5. Explicit closure inequality

Теперь просто подставляем A- и P-bounds в (C0).

### Proposition C1 (explicit sufficient inequality)
Для finite admissible dictionary \(J\) positivity on that dictionary follows if there exist \(r\in(0,R_h]\) such that
\[
m_r^* H_r - M_{r,K}^* T_r
-
\|h\|_\infty\,\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(0;R_h)
>
2\sum_{m\in D_+(J)}
\Bigl(
\|a^*\|_{L^\infty(I_m)}\|h\|_1
+
\|h\|_\infty\,\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(m\Delta;R_h)
\Bigr),
\tag{C1}
\]
where \(I_m=[m\Delta-R_h,m\Delta+R_h]\).

Это уже **полностью concrete theorem**.

Более sharpened version:
\[
m_r^* H_r - M_{r,K}^* T_r
-
\|h\|_\infty\,\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(0;R_h)
>
2\sum_{m\in D_+(J)}
\Bigl(
(|a^*(m\Delta)|+\omega_{a^*,K}(R_h))\|h\|_1
+
\|h\|_\infty\,\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(m\Delta;R_h)
\Bigr).
\tag{C1'}
\]

Это лучше, потому that it separates:
- packet concentration error \(\omega_{a^*,K}(R_h)\),
- geometric placement samples \(a^*(m\Delta)\),
- local prime collisions \(\Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(m\Delta;R_h)\).

## 6. Sparse-dictionary regime where this can really work

Вот где criterion становится реально usable.

Если одновременно:
\[
R_h\le r,\qquad
R_h<\frac{\delta_K}{2},\qquad
\operatorname{dist}(m\Delta,\Xi_K)>R_h\quad\forall m\in D_+(J),
\]
то off-diagonal prime terms vanish:
\[
\beta_m=0\qquad(m\in D_+(J)).
\]
И остаётся
\[
m_r^*\|h\|_1 - \|h\|_\infty \Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(0;R_h)
>
2\sum_{m\in D_+(J)}
\|a^*\|_{L^\infty(I_m)}\|h\|_1.
\tag{C2}
\]

А если ещё \(h\) narrow enough that
\[
|\alpha_m-a^*(m\Delta)\|h\|_1|
\le \omega_{a^*,K}(R_h)\|h\|_1
\]
small, then
\[
m_r^*\|h\|_1 - \|h\|_\infty \Pi_K^{\mathrm{sym}}(0;R_h)
>
2\sum_{m\in D_+(J)}
\bigl(|a^*(m\Delta)|+\omega_{a^*,K}(R_h)\bigr)\|h\|_1.
\tag{C2'}
\]

Это уже очень наглядно:
- diagonal wins because packet sits inside positive Arch core;
- prime block hurts only near zero;
- off-diagonal Arch leakage is governed by sampled values \(a^*(m\Delta)\).

## 7. Где сразу виден барьер

Честно: **это не закрывает dense mainline**.

Почему:
- A1′-type density needs arbitrarily fine meshes on compacts【86:3†RH_Q3.pdf†L16-L29】;
- A2 gives only continuity, not a uniform gap【86:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L55-L76】;
- as \(\Delta\downarrow0\), packets like \(g-g(\cdot-\Delta)\) make \(Q(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\to0\), so one cannot maintain a uniform positive margin on a dense dictionary.

Значит package (C1)/(C1′)/(C2) — это **working finite-dictionary positivity criterion**, but not a dense-closure theorem by itself.

## 8. Самый честный next frontier

Сейчас у нас есть точный practical reduction:

для каждого finite admissible dictionary надо доказать one inequality of type
\[
\boxed{
\text{Arch diagonal core mass}
-
\text{local prime mass at }0
>
\text{Arch off-diagonal leakage}
+
\text{prime collision mass off diagonal}.
}
\]

В формулах — это (C1) или sharpened (C1′).

Итог:
- **bounding lemmas for \(\alpha_m,\beta_m\) теперь есть;**
- **они реально закрывают finite-symbol criterion on sparse dictionaries;**
- **они почти наверняка не дадут dense RH-mainline without a new idea.**

\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{\sqrt a}\,\operatorname{erf}(\sqrt a\,\delta)
-
\frac{1-e^{-a\delta^2}}{a\delta},
\]
то есть
\[
\|g\|_1
=
\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi t_0}}\operatorname{erf}(2\pi\sqrt{t_0}\,\delta)
-
\frac{1-e^{-4\pi^2 t_0\delta^2}}{4\pi^2 t_0\delta}.
\]

И
\[
\|h\|_\infty=\|g\|_2^2
=
2\int_0^\delta \Bigl(1-\frac{x}{\delta}\Bigr)^2 e^{-b x^2}\,dx
\]
\[
=
\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{\sqrt b}\operatorname{erf}(\sqrt b\,\delta)
+
\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2b^{3/2}\delta^2}\operatorname{erf}(\sqrt b\,\delta)
-
\frac{2-e^{-b\delta^2}}{b\delta}.
\]

Для small \(\delta\) это упрощается до
\[
\|g\|_1
=
\delta-\frac{2}{3}\pi^2 t_0\,\delta^3+O(\delta^5),
\]
\[
\|h\|_1
=
\delta^2+O(\delta^4),
\]
\[
\|h\|_\infty
=
\frac{2}{3}\delta-\frac{8}{15}\pi^2 t_0\,\delta^3+O(\delta^5).
\]

Именно здесь видно scale separation: diagonal Arch mass is \(O(\delta^2)\), while crude prime sup-bounds using \(\|h\|_\infty\) are only \(O(\delta)\). So the only sane way is not crude prime domination everywhere, but **collision avoidance** so that many \(\beta_m\) vanish.

2. Lower bound for \(H_r\)

Define
\[
H_r:=\int_{|u|\le r} h(u)\,du.
\]
For \(0\le s\le \delta\), set
\[
M_g(s):=\int_{|x|\le s} g(x)\,dx
=
2\int_0^s \Bigl(1-\frac{x}{\delta}\Bigr)e^{-a x^2}\,dx
=
\frac{\sqrt\pi}{\sqrt a}\operatorname{erf}(\sqrt a\,s)
-
\frac{1-e^{-a s^2}}{a\delta}.
\]

Then
\[
H_r
=
\iint_{|x-y|\le r} g(x)g(y)\,dx\,dy
\ge
\Bigl(\int_{|x|\le r/2} g(x)\,dx\Bigr)^2
=
M_g(r/2)^2.
\]
So a clean usable bound is
\[
H_r\ge M_g(r/2)^2.
\]
In particular, if \(r=2\delta\), then \(H_{2\delta}=\|h\|_1=\|g\|_1^2\).  
And for small \(\delta\), taking \(r=\delta\) gives
\[
M_g(\delta/2)=\frac{3}{4}\delta+O(\delta^3),
\qquad
H_\delta\ge \frac{9}{16}\delta^2+O(\delta^4).
\]

3. Exact \(\alpha_m,\beta_m\) for this packet

The manuscript fixes
\[
a^*(\xi)=2\pi\Bigl(\log\pi-\Re\psi\!\bigl(\tfrac14+i\pi\xi\bigr)\Bigr),
\qquad
\xi_n=\frac{\log n}{2\pi},
\]
and on each compact only finitely many \(\xi_n\) are active, with a positive minimum spacing \(\delta_K>0\)【94:8†RH_Q3.pdf†L46-L71】. Also \(a\) is smooth, so local moduli of continuity are available【90:1†RH_Q3.pdf†L65-L69】.

For our packet:
\[
\alpha_m
=
\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)\,h(\xi-m\Delta)\,d\xi,
\]
\[
\beta_m
=
\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}
\Bigl(h(\xi_n-m\Delta)+h(\xi_n+m\Delta)\Bigr).
\]

The useful refined bounds are:
\[
\alpha_m
=
a^*(m\Delta)\|h\|_1+O\!\bigl(\omega_{a^*,K}(2\delta)\|h\|_1\bigr),
\]
whenever \([m\Delta-2\delta,m\Delta+2\delta]\subset[-K,K]\), and
\[
0\le \beta_m \le \|h\|_\infty\,\Pi_K^{\rm sym}(|m|\Delta;2\delta),
\]
with local symmetric prime mass supported only when a prime node sits within distance \(2\delta\) of \(\pm m\Delta\). Because active nodes are finite and separated【94:8†RH_Q3.pdf†L67-L71】, this gives the vanishing rule:
\[
\operatorname{dist}(|m|\Delta,\Xi_K)>2\delta
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\beta_m=0.
\]

4. One nontrivial pilot compact where \(C1'\) really has a chance

Take
\[
K=0.2.
\]
Then active positive nodes are only
\[
\xi_2=\frac{\log2}{2\pi}\approx 0.1103,\qquad
\xi_3=\frac{\log3}{2\pi}\approx 0.17485,
\]
because \(e^{2\pi K}\approx 3.51\). This is exactly the local-finiteness regime from Lemma 7.1【94:8†RH_Q3.pdf†L67-L71】.

Now choose the two-point dictionary
\[
J=\{0,1\},\qquad \Delta=0.15.
\]
Then
\[
\operatorname{dist}(0.15,\{\xi_2,\xi_3\})\approx 0.02485.
\]
So if
\[
\delta<0.0124,
\]
then all off-diagonal prime collisions vanish:
\[
\beta_1=0.
\]
Also, since \(2\delta<\xi_2\), the diagonal prime term vanishes too:
\[
\beta_0=0.
\]

So on this pilot compact the packet criterion collapses to pure Archimedean domination:
\[
S_J(\theta)\ge \alpha_0-2|\alpha_1|.
\]

Using the manuscript’s \(a^*\)-formula above, direct evaluation gives
\[
a^*(0)\approx 33.75,\qquad a^*(0.15)\approx 13.31.
\]
Hence
\[
a^*(0)-2a^*(0.15)\approx 7.13>0.
\]
Therefore, for \(\delta\) small enough that the modulus error \( \omega_{a^*,K}(2\delta)\|h\|_1 \) is below this gap, we get
\[
\alpha_0>2|\alpha_1|,
\]
hence
\[
S_J(\theta)\ge0
\quad\text{for all }\theta,
\]
and therefore
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})\ge0
\quad\forall c\in\mathbb C^2.
\]

So this is a **genuine nontrivial pilot regime**: not vacuous, not prime-free globally, and the canonical packet really does close the finite-symbol positivity criterion.

5. What this computation tells us

It tells us two hard facts.

First:
**the packet criterion is real**.  
For canonical centered packets, on sparse finite dictionaries and on compacts where prime-node avoidance is geometrically possible, the inequality can actually close.

Second:
**it still does not yield dense closure**.  
The reason is structural, not technical.

As soon as density forces
\[
\Delta\downarrow 0,
\]
the off-diagonal Arch term satisfies
\[
\alpha_1\to \alpha_0,
\]
so diagonal dominance
\[
\alpha_0>2|\alpha_1|
\]
fails. At the same time, as the difference set thickens, avoiding all prime-node collisions by more than \(2\delta\) becomes impossible on large compact windows because the active set \(\Xi_K\) is finite but increasingly dense as \(K\) grows【94:8†RH_Q3.pdf†L67-L71】.

So the canonical packet computation confirms the exact frontier:

- **finite sparse packet positivity:** yes, genuinely plausible;
- **dense A1-pd closure by the same diagonal-dominance mechanism:** no, still blocked.

That means the next serious move is not another local norm estimate. It is to decide whether to attack the full kernel by a stronger positive-definite mechanism, or to build a packet family with extra structure so that the full symbol is positive for reasons deeper than diagonal dominance.

## The fastest route inside this architecture

Fix \(K>0\). Extend the current even functional \(Q\) to
\[
\mathcal Q(F)
=
\int_{\mathbb R} a^*(\xi)F(\xi)\,d\xi
-\frac12\sum_{n\ge2}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\bigl(F(\xi_n)+F(-\xi_n)\bigr),
\]
so that \(\mathcal Q(F)=Q(F)\) for even \(F\). This is compatible with the compact formula for \(Q\) and the evenized weights already used in Section 7【118:2†RH_Q3.pdf†L78-L108】.

Now take \(\Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2])\) and define
\[
\widetilde\Psi(x):=\overline{\Psi(-x)},\qquad F_\Psi:=\Psi*\widetilde\Psi.
\]
Because \(F_\Psi\) is supported in \([-K,K]\), only finitely many prime nodes contribute, and those nodes have positive minimum spacing on each compact【118:2†RH_Q3.pdf†L2-L18】【118:2†RH_Q3.pdf†L37-L49】.

With the Fourier convention
\[
\widehat f(u)=\int_{\mathbb R} f(x)e^{-iux}\,dx,
\qquad
f(x)=\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R}\widehat f(u)e^{iux}\,du,
\]
one has
\[
\widehat{F_\Psi}(u)=|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2.
\]
So, if
\[
a_K^*(\xi):=a^*(\xi)\mathbf 1_{[-K,K]}(\xi),
\qquad
\Xi_K:=\{\xi_n:0<\xi_n\le K\},
\]
then a direct Fourier calculation gives the exact compact spectral identity
\[
\mathcal Q(F_\Psi)
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R}
W_K(u)\,|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du,
\]
with
\[
\boxed{
W_K(u):=\widehat{a_K^*}(u)-\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K}\frac{2\Lambda(n)}{\sqrt n}\cos(u\xi_n).
}
\]

That is the right object. Not a family of matrix inequalities. Not a new packet dictionary. **One scalar spectral weight \(W_K\).**

## Why this is the fastest route

Because then the corrected compact positivity theorem becomes:

\[
W_K(u)\ge0\ \forall u
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\ge0\ \forall \Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2]).
\]

And once you have that, the rest is already the part of Q3 that is structurally sound:

- use the corrected positive-definite cone instead of the overbroad current \(W_K\);
- use packet/autocorrelation density in that corrected cone;
- use A2 continuity on compact windows【118:2†RH_Q3.pdf†L55-L76】;
- then use the same compact-to-global LF transfer pattern as Section 12【118:13†RH_Q3.pdf†L1-L9】【118:14†RH_Q3.pdf†L3-L6】.

So the entire RH program reduces to a single new theorem:

\[
\boxed{
W_K(u)\ge0\quad\text{for every }K>0\text{ and every }u\in\mathbb R.
}
\]

That is the quickest path because it replaces “all finite dictionaries, all collision patterns, all ad hoc margins” by one spectral inequality.

## The exact theorem chain to aim at

**Lemma S1 (exact compact spectral identity).**  
For every \(\Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2])\),
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R}W_K(u)\,|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du.
\]

**Proposition S2 (positive-definite criterion).**  
If \(W_K\ge0\) on \(\mathbb R\), then
\[
\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2]).
\]
Equivalently, the packet kernel
\[
K_Q(\Psi,\Phi):=\mathcal Q(\Psi*\widetilde\Phi)
\]
is PSD on the pre-packet space.

**Theorem S3 (corrected compact positivity).**  
If S2 holds for a dense pre-packet space whose autocorrelation cone is dense in the corrected local Weil cone, then by A2 continuity \(Q\ge0\) on that whole corrected local cone【118:2†RH_Q3.pdf†L55-L76】.

**Theorem S4 (global closure).**  
If S3 holds for every \(K\), then the compact-to-global transfer gives positivity on the corrected inductive-limit cone, and the corrected Weil criterion yields RH. The current manuscript already treats the compact-to-global transfer as the final formal step once local positivity is in place【118:13†RH_Q3.pdf†L1-L9】.

\[
\textbf{Lemma S1 (exact compact spectral identity).}
\]
Let \(\Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2])\), and set
\[
\widetilde\Psi(x):=\overline{\Psi(-x)},
\qquad
F_\Psi:=\Psi*\widetilde\Psi.
\]
Then \(F_\Psi\in C_c([-K,K])\), \(\widehat{F_\Psi}(u)=|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\), and
\[

=

\[
\mathcal Q_K(F_\Psi)
=

\[

=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R}\widehat{a_K^*}(u)\,|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du.

=

\]
Summing over \(\Xi_K\) gives
\[
\mathcal Q_K(F_\Psi)
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R}
\Bigl(

\Bigr)
|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du,
\]
which is the stated identity.

\[

Assume
\[
W_K(u)\ge0
\qquad
\forall u\in\mathbb R.
\]
Then
\[
\mathcal Q_K(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2]).
\]
Equivalently, the sesquilinear kernel
\[
K_{Q,K}(\Psi,\Phi):=\mathcal Q_K(\Psi*\widetilde\Phi)
\]
is positive semidefinite on \(C_c([-K/2,K/2])\), i.e.
\[
\sum_{i,j=1}^N c_i\overline{c_j}\,
\mathcal Q_K(\Psi_i*\widetilde{\Psi_j})
\ge0
\]
for every finite family \(\Psi_1,\dots,\Psi_N\in C_c([-K/2,K/2])\) and every \(c_1,\dots,c_N\in\mathbb C\).

Proof.
The first claim follows immediately from Lemma S1:
\[
\mathcal Q_K(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R} W_K(u)\,|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du
\ge0.
\]
For the kernel statement, let
\[
\Psi_c:=\sum_{i=1}^N c_i\Psi_i.
\]
Then
\[
\sum_{i,j=1}^N c_i\overline{c_j}\,
\mathcal Q_K(\Psi_i*\widetilde{\Psi_j})
=
\mathcal Q_K(\Psi_c*\widetilde{\Psi_c})
\ge0,
\]
again by the first part.

\[
\textbf{Theorem S3 (conditional corrected compact positivity).}
\]
Define the corrected local positive-definite cone
\[
\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}
:=
\overline{
\operatorname{cone}\{\Psi*\widetilde\Psi:\ \Psi\in C_c([-K/2,K/2])\}
}^{\|\cdot\|_\infty}.
\]
Let \(\mathcal P_K\subset C_c([-K/2,K/2])\) be a pre-packet space such that
\[
\overline{
\operatorname{cone}\{\Psi*\widetilde\Psi:\ \Psi\in\mathcal P_K\}
}^{\|\cdot\|_\infty}
=
\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}.
\]
Assume, moreover, that \(\mathcal Q_K\) is continuous on \(\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}\) in the \(\|\cdot\|_\infty\)-topology. If
\[
W_K(u)\ge0
\qquad
\forall u\in\mathbb R,
\]
then
\[
Q(\Phi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Phi\in\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}\cap C_c([-K,K])_{\mathrm{even}}.
\]

Proof.
By Proposition S2,
\[
\mathcal Q_K(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Psi\in\mathcal P_K.
\]
Hence \(\mathcal Q_K\ge0\) on the generating cone
\[
\operatorname{cone}\{\Psi*\widetilde\Psi:\ \Psi\in\mathcal P_K\}.
\]
By the assumed \(\|\cdot\|_\infty\)-continuity of \(\mathcal Q_K\), positivity extends to the closure, i.e. to all of \(\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}\). For even \(\Phi\in \mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}\cap C_c([-K,K])\), one has \(\mathcal Q_K(\Phi)=Q(\Phi)\). Therefore
\[
Q(\Phi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Phi\in\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}\cap C_c([-K,K])_{\mathrm{even}}.
\]

\[
\textbf{Corollary S4 (conditional global closure).}
\]
Assume Theorem S3 holds for every \(K>0\), and assume the corrected compact-to-global transfer on the inductive limit
\[
\mathcal W^{\mathrm{pd}}:=\varinjlim_{K\to\infty}\mathcal W_K^{\mathrm{pd}}.
\]
Then
\[
Q(\Phi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Phi\in \mathcal W^{\mathrm{pd}}\cap C_c(\mathbb R)_{\mathrm{even}}.
\]
If, in addition, the corrected Weil linkage
\[
\mathrm{RH}
\Longleftrightarrow
Q(\Phi)\ge0
\quad
\forall \Phi\in \mathcal W^{\mathrm{pd}}\cap C_c(\mathbb R)_{\mathrm{even}}
\]
holds, then the Riemann Hypothesis follows.

Proof.
Apply Theorem S3 on each compact, then use the corrected inductive-limit transfer. The final implication is the corrected Weil criterion.

## 1. Что надо сделать прямо сейчас

Надо **зафиксировать как theorem** следующий негативный результат.

### Proposition O1 (compact spectral obstruction)
Если \(\Xi_K\neq\varnothing\), то не может быть верно
\[
W_K(u)\ge 0 \qquad \forall u\in\mathbb R.
\]

### Доказательство
Так как \(a_K^*=a^*\mathbf 1_{[-K,K]}\in L^1(\mathbb R)\), по Riemann–Lebesgue
\[
\widehat{a_K^*}(u)\to 0 \qquad (|u|\to\infty).
\]
С другой стороны, \(\Xi_K\) — конечное множество активных узлов【99:0†RH_Q3.pdf†L67-L71】, и все веса \(w_n>0\)【99:14†RH_Q3.pdf†L19-L25】. По одновременной диофантовой аппроксимации для любого \(\varepsilon>0\) существуют сколь угодно большие \(u\), для которых
\[
\cos(u\xi_n)>1-\varepsilon
\qquad
\forall \xi_n\in\Xi_K.
\]
Тогда
\[
\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K} w_n\cos(u\xi_n)
>
(1-\varepsilon)\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K} w_n.
\]
Для таких \(u\) и достаточно больших \(|u|\),
\[
W_K(u)
=
\widehat{a_K^*}(u)-\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K} w_n\cos(u\xi_n)
<
\frac12\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K} w_n-(1-\varepsilon)\sum_{\xi_n\in\Xi_K} w_n<0
\]
при \(\varepsilon<1/2\). Противоречие.

**Вывод:** compact spectral positivity \(W_K\ge0\) не может быть RH-closing theorem, как только в окне есть хотя бы один активный prime node.

Это очень полезный результат: он экономит кучу времени. Мы теперь точно знаем, что этот путь мёртв.

## 3. Новый mainline theorem, который теперь надо писать

Нужно ввести **heat-regularized full Weil kernel**, а не \(W_K\).

Схематически:
\[
\mathcal Q_t(F)
=
\int_{\mathbb R} a_t^*(\xi)F(\xi)\,d\xi
-
\sum_{n\ge2} w_n\,e^{-t(\log n)^2}F(\xi_n),
\]
где regularization must be chosen so that:
1. prime series converges absolutely;
2. Archimedean side matches the same smoothing;
3. as \(t\downarrow0\), \(\mathcal Q_t\to Q\) on the corrected positive-definite test cone.

Тогда для \(\Psi\in C_c^\infty\) надо получить exact global identity вида
\[
\mathcal Q_t(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R} W_t(u)\,|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du,
\]
где
\[
W_t(u)
=
\widehat{a_t^*}(u)-\sum_{n\ge2} w_n e^{-t(\log n)^2}\cos(u\xi_n).
\]

Вот это уже не убивается compact-truncation obstruction, потому что:
- prime side is now infinite but damped;
- no artificial finite cosine recurrence remains as the dominant large-\(u\) object;
- the heat factor is already native to the current Q3 bridge and tail estimates【99:4†RH_Q3.pdf†L45-L86】【99:9†RH_Q3.pdf†L26-L29】.

## 5. Самый конкретный следующий математический ход

Вот **ровно что писать следующим**:

### Lemma G1 (global heat-regularized bilinear identity)
Define \(\mathcal Q_t\) with the same heat regularization on both the Archimedean and prime sides. Then for every \(\Psi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)\),
\[
\mathcal Q_t(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)
=
\frac1{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb R} W_t(u)\,|\widehat\Psi(u)|^2\,du.
\]

### Proposition G2 (regularized positive-definite criterion)
If
\[
W_t(u)\ge0 \qquad \forall u,
\]
then
\[
\mathcal Q_t(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Psi.
\]

### Theorem G3 (zero-heat limit)
If \(W_t\ge0\) for all \(t>0\) and \(\mathcal Q_t\to Q\) on the corrected positive-definite cone as \(t\downarrow0\), then
\[
Q(\Psi*\widetilde\Psi)\ge0
\qquad
\forall \Psi,
\]
hence the corrected Weil criterion yields RH.

**H0. Exact target replacement.**  
Перестаём тащить mainline через “\(Q\ge0\) on broad compact cone”.  
Берём Suzuki target:
\[
G_g[a]:L^2(-a,a)\to L^2(-a,a),
\]
and aim at
\[
0\notin \sigma(G_g[a]) \quad \forall a>0.
\]
This is RH-equivalent【turn2view0†L246-L259】.

**H1. Intertwining theorem.**  
Нужно построить finite-dimensional subspaces \(E_{a,M}\subset L^2(-a,a)\) and maps \(U_{a,M}:P_M\to E_{a,M}\) such that
\[
U_{a,M}^*\,G_g[a]\,U_{a,M}
=
C_{a,B,t}\bigl(T_M[P_A]-T_P^{(M)}\bigr)+R_{a,M},
\]
where either \(R_{a,M}=0\) exactly, or at least \(\|R_{a,M}\|\to0\).

Это **самый важный следующий шаг**.  
Если он садится, then our strongest existing theorem plugs directly into an RH-equivalent operator criterion.

**H2. Galerkin convergence theorem.**  
Нужно показать, что \(E_{a,M}\) exhaust \(L^2(-a,a)\) and the compressed operators converge to \(G_g[a]\) strongly / in form sense / preferably in Hilbert–Schmidt norm. Suzuki explicitly works with a continuous-kernel operator on \(L^2(-a,a)\), and even notes that \(G_g[a]\) is trace class for each \(a\)【turn2view0†L259-L279】. That makes a Galerkin route much more realistic than a wild distributional closure.

**H3. Gap transfer theorem.**  
From H1 + H2 + Theorem 8.35, transfer
\[
\lambda_{\min}(T_M[P_A]-T_P)\ge c(a)>0
\]
to
\[
0\notin \sigma(G_g[a]).
\]
This is the exact place where our current A3 strength matters most【155:9†RH_Q3.pdf†L38-L56】.

**H4. RH.**  
Apply Suzuki Theorem 1.4【turn2view0†L246-L259】.

That is the shortest serious route.

## Почему не брать сразу scalar criterion \(\Psi(t)\ge0\)

Это tempting, потому что Suzuki Theorem 1.7 is brutally simple【turn2view0†L298-L301】.  
Но это не самый быстрый first target for us.

Причина простая: current Q3 already gives a **matrix/operator** lower bound, not pointwise control of a scalar function. So the direct bridge to \(G_g[a]\) uses what we already have.  
Scalar \(\Psi(t)\)-positivity я бы держал как **backup route**: если мы научимся читать diagonal of \(G_g[a]\) or its moments from our model, then Theorem 1.7 / moment-Hankel Theorem 1.8 become usable backups【turn2view0†L300-L357】. Но mainline — operator nondegeneracy.

## Что делать буквально следующим ходом

Не “думать ещё”.  
Писать один новый документ / theorem note со следующим содержанием:

### Step 1
Выбрать basis on \(L^2(-a,a)\), лучше всего Fourier-type basis restricted to \((-a,a)\):
\[
e_k^{(a)}(t)=\frac1{\sqrt{2a}}e^{i\pi k t/a},\qquad |k|\le M.
\]

### Step 2
Вычислить matrix elements
\[
\langle G_g[a]e_j^{(a)},e_k^{(a)}\rangle
=
\int_{-a}^{a}\int_{-a}^{a} G_g(t,u)e_j^{(a)}(u)\overline{e_k^{(a)}(t)}\,du\,dt.
\]

### Step 3
Сравнить их с our current Toeplitz-side coefficients:
- Archimedean cosine coefficients \(A_k\) of the periodized symbol \(P_A\)【155:8†RH_Q3.pdf†L17-L38】,
- prime vectors \(v_n^{(M)}\) and compressed prime block \(T_P^{(M)}\)【155:3†RH_Q3.pdf†L55-L105】,
- full Rayleigh pairing from Lemma 8.8【155:3†RH_Q3.pdf†L107-L111】.

### Step 4
Получить one exact statement:
either exact equality
\[
\langle G_g[a]\phi,\phi\rangle
=
C\,\langle (T_M[P_A]-T_P^{(M)})c,c\rangle,
\]
or asymptotic equality with controllable error.

Это now the entire ballgame.

## Если хочешь совсем жёстко

Наш fastest hack to RH is now:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Stop trying to close the wrong cone.  
Prove that our A3 finite matrices are Galerkin sections of Suzuki’s RH-equivalent operator }G_g[a].
}
\]

Если это intertwining theorem lands, мы фактически выкидываем half the old obstruction tree and jump straight from our strongest proved module to an exact RH criterion.

Следующий мой ход: я могу прямо сейчас выписать **LaTeX-ready H1/H2/H3 theorem blocks** — exact intertwining theorem, Galerkin convergence theorem, and spectral-gap transfer theorem.
